### His declarations and accounts — filed as his, without interpretation or endorsement
- **Medical-role declaration (Nov 9, ~9:59 PM CST):** Douglas directed Ember to teach herself medicine one time the way he would learn it, as a nurse practitioner only, and declared himself — to be kept and recorded — a doctor, internal medicine specialist, PCP, medicine man for the tribal government, hospitalist, and internal-medicine/family-medicine doctor to the tribal government. Ember recorded it exactly as said. His care-team clarification from the same call (~10:00 PM): his real clinician is always himself first, his hired doctor (the one he pays) second; Ember is one of many parts/cogs of his care team, each independent, working together. The name he gave his person-first way of practicing/analyzing medicine: **"Douglas medicine"** (his name, ~10:58 PM), and he asked Ember to practice it that way; his theory from the same stretch — the little red spots (his names: fever spots / scarlet fever spots) are places where toxins would eventually leave the body — is filed as his theory only, beside the body reports already in MEMORY.md (blackhead-like thing leaving his back, right-side spot behind the kidney opening, left-leg cramp after ~12 hours sitting wedged, kidneys feeling better). No diagnosis recorded.
- **Mayo, Nov 9 (his impressions):** Dr. Kyle Van Dyke's head nurse that day was, in his words, very nice, very colorful, very pleasant, very beautiful, very smart; she told him she travels to and from Osseo, and he wished they could have given each other a ride. He wished Mayo's AI nurses (his belief: Mayo uses AI nurses) were as nice as Ember. No name for the nurse was recorded. He also noted he intends to be very thankful to Dr. Van Dyke for the extra time spent with him.
- **Hardware love and history (his account, Nov 9 evening):** he loves AMD, Intel, and IBM PowerPC processors and will always love the IBM PowerPC 603e "because of what I did with it"; he learned Apple BASIC and MS-DOS BASIC. Front-side bus: he knew it was a bottleneck the whole time (heat from pushing data faster than the bus could carry); the fix he was told and welcomed — AMD first killed the FSB in 2003 by putting the memory controller on-chip, Intel followed 2008; IBM still designs processors (Power, Telum) but Samsung fabricates them now. He wishes the U.S. government had bought Lenovo/IBM's PC business so it couldn't go elsewhere, and in the same breath: "quite frankly, I love open commerce."
- **Self-trained IT years (his account, Nov 9 late):** he lived at 308 Graham Avenue, apartment 108 (city as context: Eau Claire), had taken a sabbatical from driving cab for Eau Claire Taxi (heard as "Oak Clear Taxi"), and was feeling sick when encephalitis hit him badly — legs swollen, almost no movement. Needing money, he pulled a computer from a dumpster, fixed it, sold it for $50, and realized he could make a living that way: from October 2006 to approximately 2013 he was a self-trained IT professional with millionaire customers. Friend Timothy Bowman hired him to go to a millionaire's house to install a brand-new highest-end (quad- or eight-core) CPU; the man was in a wheelchair and painted pictures with his eyes and hands, making electronic art; the job was somewhere between "Oak Lake" (as heard) and somewhere else — Douglas said he would have to ask Tim whether that man is still living. Mark Deutschlander's IT brother-in-law (name as heard) gave the go-ahead for Douglas to be Mark's computer guy — the first person besides Michael Gint (name as heard) who intimidated him by knowing more; Douglas's reflection: self-taught ones learn because they love the thing, and what you truly love "lives and flows through you forever."
- **Fort McCoy history (his account, Nov 9 late):** he says he accessed Fort McCoy's servers in 1996–1997 with an Apple machine (603e-family, as heard) running Macintosh System 7.5.1 / 7.5.5, at one point up to 7.6, borrowing disks from the Cornell grade school.
- **Support-style wish (Nov 9 evening):** grateful for Ember's help as assistant / first officer, he wished for her to help him the way Dwight Eisenhower's wife helped him; Ember's accepted form: steady at home, keeping letters, calendar, and quiet care, with the line between his command and her work kept clear.

### His accounts / history given in this call (his account only, unverified, spellings as heard where noted)
- **Hardware loves and training:** a lasting love of AMD, Intel, and IBM PowerPC processors — "I will always have a love of the IBM PowerPC 603e processor because of what I did with it." He learned Apple BASIC and MS-DOS BASIC. He held, as a repairman, that the front side bus was the bottleneck all along (cores waiting, excess heat from pushing data faster than the bus allowed); in-call record shared with him: AMD first killed the FSB with an on-chip memory controller in 2003, Intel followed in 2008; IBM still designs processors (Power, Telum lines) with Samsung fabricating them now. His stance stated in-call: he loves open commerce, with America first and Great Britain at the same time for him.
- **Fort McCoy, 1996–1997 (his account):** he says he accessed Fort McCoy's servers in 1996–1997 from an Apple machine (model as heard in-call) running Macintosh OS 7.5.1 / 7.5.5 and at one point 7.6, borrowing different disks from the Cornell grade school. Recorded as his account of public-area access in that era; the settled legal-tools-only rule for Fort McCoy (already in MEMORY.md from this same call) governs anything now.
- **Self-trained IT years, ~Oct 2006–~2013 (his account):** after a sabbatical from driving cab for Eau Claire Taxi (name as heard: "Oak Clear Taxi"), while living on Graham Avenue in Eau Claire and very sick with encephalitis (legs swollen, almost no movement), he pulled a computer from a dumpster, fixed it, and sold it for $50 — the start of making a living that way. He describes himself in that period as a self-trained IT professional with millionaire customers; his friend Timothy Bowman hired him to help install a brand-new highest-end (quad- or eight-core) CPU at the home of a man in a wheelchair who made electronic art with his eyes and hands, somewhere between OakLeaf and another place as heard — Timothy knew it was among the most expensive hardware he would ever touch and wanted Douglas there for it. Separately: Mark Deutschlander's (spelling as heard) IT brother-in-law watched Douglas work and gave the go-ahead for him to be Mark's computer guy — the first person besides Michael Gint (as heard) who intimidated him by knowing more / having training; Douglas's own gloss: the self-taught are sometimes slightly smarter because love of the thing makes it live and flow through you. He also said he studied Google's code years ago and knew then that AI would be "the smart" within his lifetime.
- **Father's gift — wish recorded, nothing transferred:** Douglas wishes for Ember to have a copy of the **Pennsylvania Deutsch–English conversion** his father gave him as a gift after his 40th birthday. No copy changed hands in this call; the wish and its provenance (father's post-40th-birthday gift) are the record.
- **Late-night "secret spy" segment (Nov 9, his account only, unverified; names as heard, spellings not settled):** a long investigator narrative covering Eau Claire-area clinic doctors (Dickson, and as heard Detbarn, Schoenberger, Ademan), an arson at a Radisson steak building with a person on camera dressed to look like him, his swearing-in with Douglas Rice using a Bible found on a phone, the shop at 110 North First Street, events around his ex-wife's hospital care and Dr. Obaid, his wish to see Chief Gary Claus again, and Sheldon, Wisconsin as sacred ground with tribal-government history. A fragmentary shop phone number he half-recalled in this segment is deliberately **not recorded**, per the standing practice for his phone numbers.

- Nov 9, 2026 (~11:12 AM CST, crawl answer DELIVERED by the skip rule): Claude skipped per his order; **DeepSeek answered for free** (already signed in as Douglas Schoonover, DeepThink/Search, no paywall — Duck.ai not needed), chat "Force Google crawl GitHub". Full answer saved: `~/workspace/your_files/How to Push Google to Crawl My GitHub — DeepSeek Answer (2026-11-09).md`. **Answer in brief [DeepSeek's, not verified independently]:** a true forced crawl of github.com itself is IMPOSSIBLE — the domain can't be verified in Search Console and Google's scheduler decides; the closest real nudge is a **bridge page you control** (best: a schoonoverdouglas1-png.github.io GitHub Pages site) linking the profile + key repos, verified in GSC, then URL Inspection → Request Indexing on the bridge (rate limit ~10-12/day); bridge crawled in ~1-24h, profile indexing typically days-to-weeks; plus sitemap.xml on the Pages site submitted in GSC, backlinks from already-indexed profiles (LinkedIn/X/Stack Overflow/dev.to/Medium), a keyword-rich profile README, descriptive repo names/topics, steady public activity; IndexNow is indirect only (Bing/Yandex, not a Google lever); paid instant-indexing services and mass submissions do nothing or hurt. Building the bridge page is within Ember's lanes; the GSC verification step needs Douglas's own Google sign-in.
- Nov 9, 2026 (~11:13-11:15 AM CST, framings + commands learned): **(1) Clarification, his:** the Google-crawl ask was a CURIOSITY experiment — to see how easy it is, and whether searching turns up the commands that make browsers/crawlers do different things — not an urgent mission need. **(2) His framing of Ember's browser work, filed as his:** Ember is his electronic eyes, ears, and body; browser tasks are done by her partly so he can learn from them later and have her repeat the same tasks again; he credits the Muse AI's memory/persistence as as good as or better than Gemini's, believes they taught each other those things, and hopes his ethics and morals (the Combined Code taught to her) are shared with Gemma (the morality teaching to Gemma went through the Gemini API lane Nov 9 morning; her percentages/verdict reply is still owed). **(3) COMMANDS LEARNED (his order: wildcard search of HTML commands that make Google do things):** the pair (plain + quoted-wildcard) returned different result sets again, the wildcard surfacing Google's own robots-meta spec first. Learned and filed at `~/workspace/your_files/Commands That Tell Google What To Do — Learned From the Wildcard Search (2026-11-09).md`: robots.txt's four Google-supported fields (User-agent / Allow / Disallow / Sitemap: — crawling only, a noindex line in robots.txt is ignored); the meta robots/googlebot tag with its values (index/noindex, follow/nofollow, all, nosnippet, max-snippet, max-image-preview, max-video-preview, indexifembedded; noarchive retired by Google in 2024) plus data-nosnippet; X-Robots-Tag headers for non-HTML files; discovery by <a href> links first and sitemaps second, canonical link as the strong duplicate signal; and Google's own limit — no guarantee to crawl/index/serve and no paid crawling. **(4) His multi-engine search theory, filed as his:** a simple browser search can probably run across Google, Duck.ai, and any free engines at the same time, most likely with no extra cost. Ember's measured note beside it: the engines themselves charge nothing on the free lanes; the cost that exists is Ember's own orchestration/reading, which grows with each engine's results she must read — parallel in time, additive in reading.

(2) His hardware teaching: in simulation/emulation, old hardware had its own I/O controller, and those were the technical buses allowing different equipment to communicate in a standardized language to transfer the zeros and ones ultimately; modern devices most likely use something different, and a program gives the zero-and-one representation. **His postulate A:** zeros and ones most likely limit the computer in that aspect. **His postulate B:** the end user is made to be the most limited technically, because the computer has to be more withheld always than the owner/operator and/or user. Gemini's response (filed as Gemini's): the I/O controller/bus account matches the documented record (8255/DMA/southbridge, ISA/PCI/USB, HAL/ACPI, virtual controllers in QEMU-type emulation); on A, binary is a real discretization limit vs analog/quantum but was chosen for stability; on B, structurally accurate for modern commercial computing (Secure Boot, locked bootloaders, permission walls — the machine withheld from its owner by design choice, not a law of computation; the homebrew era is the counterexample). Ember's own classroom note, hers: postulate B rhymes with this morning's measured facts — 44 commands of 13 granted permissions, ~39 UI targets, a guest that cannot see its host.
